\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{W_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}W_{i}e^{\lambda W_{i}}}{\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda W_{i}}},

\displaystyle\var\widetilde{W_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}W_{i}^{2}e^{\lambda W_{i}}}{\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda W_{i}}}-(\mathbb{E}\widetilde{W_{i}})^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\widetilde{W_{i}}|^{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}|W_{i}|^{3}e^{\lambda W_{i}}}{\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda W_{i}}}.

We establish the expansion of the above moments in the Lemma below. The proof of Lemma
A.1
will be given in Section
B.1
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
W_{i}=2xX_{i}-x^{2}Y_{i}^{2}
. For
\frac{1}{4}\leq\lambda\leq\frac{3}{4}
and
x>0
satisfying (
2.7
), there exists an absolute constant
A
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda W_{i}}

\displaystyle=1+2\lambda^{2}x^{2}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2}-\lambda x^{2}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\lambda^{3}x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-2\lambda^{2}x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}+O_{1}R_{x,i},

(A.8)

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{2\lambda^{2}x^{2}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2}-\lambda x^{2}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\lambda^{3}x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-2\lambda^{2}x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}+O_{2}R_{x,i}\right\},

\mathbb{E}W_{i}e^{\lambda W_{i}}=4\lambda x^{2}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2}-x^{2}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}+4\lambda^{2}x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-4\lambda x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}+O_{3}R_{x,i},

(A.9)

\mathbb{E}W_{i}^{2}e^{\lambda W_{i}}=4x^{2}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2}+8\lambda x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-4x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}+O_{4}R_{x,i},

(A.10)

\mathbb{E}|W_{i}|^{3}e^{\lambda W_{i}}=O_{5}x^{3}\left(\mathbb{E}|X_{i}|^{3}+\mathbb{E}|Y_{i}|^{3}\right)+O_{6}R_{x,i},

(A.11)

where
|O_{i}|\leq A
for
i=1,\ldots,6
.

The next lemma is Lemma 2.1 in
Wang 2011
with some modifications.

Lemma A.2
.

We have for
x
satisfying (
2.7
) that

\displaystyle(1+x)^{4}(\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta_{x,i},

\displaystyle(1+x)^{5}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}|X_{i}|^{3}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta_{x,i},

\displaystyle(1+x)^{6}(\mathbb{E}|X_{i}|^{3})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{x,i},

and similar results hold for
Y_{i}
. In addition, if
x
also satisfies (
2.6
), then

(1+x)^{4}L_{3,n}^{2}\leq 2\delta_{x}.

By Lemmas
A.1
and
A.2
, it is readily seen that under (
2.7
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{W_{i}}

\displaystyle=x^{2}\left(4\lambda\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2}-\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}\right)+x^{3}\left(4\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-4\lambda\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\right)+O_{1}R_{x,i},

\displaystyle\var\widetilde{W_{i}}

\displaystyle=4x^{2}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2}+x^{3}\left(8\lambda\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-4\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\right)+O_{2}R_{x,i},

and

\mathbb{E}\big|\widetilde{W_{i}}\big|^{3}=O_{3}x^{3}\left(\mathbb{E}\left|X_{i}\right|^{3}+\mathbb{E}\left|Y_{i}\right|^{3}\right)+O_{4}R_{x,i}.

Put
m_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\widetilde{W_{i}}
,
\sigma_{n}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}Var\widetilde{W_{i}}
,
v_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big|\widetilde{W_{i}}\big|^{3}
. Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle m_{n}

\displaystyle=\left(4\lambda-1\right)x^{2}+x^{3}\left(4\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-4\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\right)+O_{1}R_{x},

(A.12)

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=4x^{2}+x^{3}\left(8\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-4\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\right)+O_{2}R_{x},

(A.13)

and

v_{n}=O_{3}x^{3}L_{3,n}+O_{4}R_{x}.

(A.14)

Define
m\left(\lambda\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log{\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda W_{i}}}
, therefore,
m_{n}=m^{\prime}(\lambda)
and
\sigma_{n}^{2}=m^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)
. Before proving Proposition
5.2
, let us present the following lemma that will be applied in the proof.

Lemma A.3
.

For
x
satisfying (
2.6
) and (
2.7
), if
|\delta\left(x\right)|<\frac{1}{2}x^{2}
and
|\delta_{0}\left(x\right)|<\frac{1}{2}x^{2}
, then the equation

m^{\prime}\left(\lambda\right)=x^{2}+\delta\left(x\right),

has a unique solution
\lambda_{\delta}
. In addition,
\lambda_{\delta}
satisfies
\frac{1}{4}<\lambda_{\delta}<\frac{3}{4}
and

\left|\lambda_{\delta}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\delta(x)}{4x^{2}}\right)+x\left(\lambda_{\delta}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-\lambda_{\delta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\right)\right|\leq Ax^{-2}R_{x},

(A.15)

and

\left|\lambda_{\delta}-\lambda_{\delta_{0}}-\frac{\delta\left(x\right)-\delta_{0}(x)}{4x^{2}}\right|\leq A\{x^{-2}R_{x}+\left|\delta(x)-\delta_{0}(x)\right|x^{-1}L_{3,n}\}.

(A.16)

Moreover, we have

m\left(\lambda_{\delta}\right)=\left(2\lambda_{\delta}^{2}-\lambda_{\delta}\right)x^{2}+x^{3}\left(\frac{4}{3}\lambda_{\delta}^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-2\lambda_{\delta}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\right)+O_{1}R_{x},

(A.17)

and

\left|m\left(\lambda_{\delta}\right)-m\left(\lambda_{\delta_{0}}\right)\right|\leq A\left(R_{x}+\left|\delta\left(x\right)-\delta_{0}\left(x\right)\right|\right).

(A.18)

The proof of Lemma
A.3
will be shown in Section
B.2
. Now we are ready to prove Proposition
5.2
.
The main idea is to use the conjugate method (see (4.9) in
Petrov 1965
).
Assume
\lambda_{1}
is the solution to
m^{\prime}(\lambda)=x^{2}+2xc
.
Write
\widetilde{S_{n}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{W_{i}}
,
U_{n}=\left(\widetilde{S_{n}}-m_{n}\right)/\sigma_{n}
. It is well-known that by the conjugate method

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(2xS_{n}-x^{2}V_{n}^{2}\geq x^{2}+2xc\right)=

\displaystyle\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})\}\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\widetilde{S_{n}}}\mathbbm{1}(\widetilde{S_{n}}\geq x^{2}+2xc)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})-\lambda_{1}m_{n}\}\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}U_{n}}\mathbbm{1}(U_{n}\geq 0).

Let
Z
be a standard normal random variable. Define

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{x\in\mathbbm{R}}|\mathbb{P}(U_{n}\leq x)-\mathbb{P}(Z\leq x)|,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}Z}\mathbbm{1}(Z\geq 0).

Integration by parts gives

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}U_{n}}\mathbbm{1}(U_{n}\geq 0)-\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}Z}\mathbbm{1}(Z\geq 0)\big|\leq 2I_{1}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}\left(2xS_{n}-x^{2}V_{n}^{2}\geq x^{2}+2xc\right)-\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})-\lambda_{1}m_{n}\}I_{2}\big|

(A.19)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq 2\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})-\lambda_{1}m_{n}\}I_{1}.

Applying the Berry-Esseen theorem to
I_{1}
and by the fact of (
A.13
) and (
A.14
), it is easy to find that for
x>3
satisfying (
2.6
),

I_{1}\leq A\frac{v_{n}}{\sigma_{n}^{3}}\leq A(L_{3,n}+x^{-3}R_{x}).

(A.20)

Regarding
I_{2}
, we have

I_{2}=\frac{e^{\lambda_{1}^{2}\sigma_{n}^{2}/2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}}^{\infty}e^{-t^{2}/2}dt=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}{1-\Phi(\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n})\over\varphi(\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n})},

where
\phi(x)
is the standard normal density function.
Let
\psi(x)=\frac{1-\Phi(x)}{\varphi(x)}
. For
x>3
,

x^{-1}/2\leq\psi(x)\leq x^{-1}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad|\psi^{\prime}(x)|=|x\psi(x)-1|\leq x^{-2}.

(A.21)

By (
A.13
) and the fact that
1/4<\lambda_{1}<3/4
,

|\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}-2\lambda_{1}x|=\frac{\lambda_{1}|\sigma_{n}^{2}-4x^{2}|}{\sigma_{n}+2x}\leq A\left(x^{2}L_{3n}+x^{-1}R_{x}\right),

therefore, under (
2.6
) for small constant
c_{1}
, there exists some
\theta^{*}
between
2\lambda_{1}x
and
\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}
, such that

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\{\psi\left(2\lambda_{1}x\right)+\psi^{\prime}\left(\theta^{*}\right)\left(\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}-2\lambda_{1}x\right)\}

(A.22)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\psi\left(2\lambda_{1}x\right)\left\{1+O_{1}(xL_{3,n}+x^{-2}R_{x})\right\}

\displaystyle=e^{2\lambda_{1}^{2}x^{2}}\left[1-\Phi\left(2\lambda_{1}x\right)\right]\left\{1+O_{1}(xL_{3,n}+x^{-2}R_{x})\right\}.

Since
1/4<\lambda_{1}<3/4
, we have for
x>3

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda_{1}^{2}x^{2}}\left[1-\Phi\left(2\lambda_{1}x\right)\right]\geq Ax^{-1},

which yields

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq Ae^{2\lambda_{1}^{2}x^{2}}\left[1-\Phi\left(2\lambda_{1}x\right)\right](xL_{3,n}+x^{-2}R_{x}).

Consequently, by (
A.19
)–(
A.22
) and
m_{n}=m^{\prime}(\lambda_{1})=x^{2}+2xc
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(2x

\displaystyle S_{n}-x^{2}V_{n}^{2}\geq x^{2}+2xc)

(A.23)

\displaystyle=\exp\big\{m(\lambda_{1})+(2\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{1})x^{2}-2\lambda_{1}xc\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[1-\Phi(2\lambda_{1}x)\right]\left\{1+O_{1}xL_{3,n}+O_{2}x^{-2}R_{x}\right\}

\displaystyle=\exp\big\{m(\lambda_{1})+\frac{1}{2}(x+c)^{2}-\lambda_{1}(x^{2}+2xc)\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]e^{O_{1}R_{x}}\{1+O_{2}xL_{3,n}\},

and the last equality holds because

|\lambda_{1}-\gamma|\leq A_{1}xL_{3,n}+A_{2}x^{-2}R_{x}.

By applying (
A.15
) and (
A.17
) in Lemma
A.3
, where
\gamma=(1+c/x)/2
. Noticing that
x^{4}L_{3,n}^{2}\leq 2\delta_{x}<2R_{x}
by Lemma
A.2
, we obtain for
x
satisfying (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) that

(2\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{1})x^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(x+c)^{2}-\lambda_{1}(x^{2}+2xc)=2x^{2}(\lambda_{1}-\gamma)^{2}\leq O_{1}R_{x}.

Thus (
A.17
) implies

\displaystyle\;m(\lambda_{1})+\frac{1}{2}(x+c)^{2}-\lambda_{1}(x^{2}+2xc)

(A.24)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{4}{3}\gamma^{3}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-2\gamma^{2}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}+O_{1}R_{x}.

Finally, we arrive at the desired result (
5.6
) by substituting (
A.24
) into (
A.23
). The proof is completed.

A.3
Proof of Proposition
5.3

The proof again relies on the conjugate method and a randomized concentration inequality for independent random variables. Recalling the definitions of
\lambda_{1},\widetilde{S_{n}}
and
U_{n}
at the very beginning of Section
A.2
, we have by the conjugate method

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(2xS_{n}-x^{2}V_{n}^{2}\geq x^{2}+2xc-x\Delta_{n}\right)

(A.25)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})\}\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\widetilde{S_{n}}}\mathbbm{1}(\widetilde{S_{n}}\geq x^{2}+2xc-x\widetilde{\Delta}_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})-\lambda_{1}m_{n}\}\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}U_{n}}\mathbbm{1}(U_{n}\geq-\frac{x\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}}{\sigma_{n}})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt:=P_{1}+P_{2},

where

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle=\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})-\lambda_{1}m_{n}\}\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}U_{n}}\mathbbm{1}(U_{n}>0),

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle=\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})-\lambda_{1}m_{n}\}\mathbb{E}e^{-\lambda_{1}\sigma_{n}U_{n}}\mathbbm{1}(-\frac{x\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\leq U_{n}\leq 0),

and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}=\min\{2x(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}^{2}-1)^{2},x^{-1}(2\lor 72R_{x})\}
. We already established in (
A.23
) that

\displaystyle P_{1}

\displaystyle=\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})+\left(2\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{1}\right)x^{2}-2\lambda_{1}xc\}

(A.26)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times[1-\Phi(2\lambda_{1}x)](1+O_{1}xL_{3,n}+O_{2}x^{-2}R_{x}).

As for
P_{2}
, because
x\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}\leq 2+72R_{x}
,

P_{2}\leq e^{2+72R_{x}}\exp\{m\left(\lambda_{1}\right)-\lambda_{1}m_{n}\}\mathbb{P}\Big(-\frac{x\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\leq U_{n}\leq 0\Big).

(A.27)

As
U_{n}
is a normalized sum of independent random variables, we apply the randomized concentration inequality by
Shao and Zhou 2016
(Theorem 4.1 therein) to estimate the concentration probability involved in
P_{2}
. As
\Delta_{n}\leq 2x(V_{n}^{2}-1)^{2}
, it is sufficient to bound the concentration probability as if
\Delta_{n}=2x(V_{n}^{2}-1)^{2}
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}=2x(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}^{2}-1)^{2}
. Denote
Z_{i}=Y_{i}^{2}-\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}
and define
\Delta_{n}^{(i)}
as
\Delta_{n}^{(i)}=2x(\sum_{j\neq i}Z_{j})^{2}
.
\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{(i)}
is defined in the same manner as
\Delta_{n}^{(i)}
but with
\{Y_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
replaced by
\{\eta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
. We obtain

\mathbb{P}\Big(-\frac{x\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}}{\sigma_{n}}\leq U_{n}\leq 0\Big)\leq 17\frac{v_{n}}{\sigma_{n}^{3}}+\frac{5x}{\sigma_{n}}\mathbb{E}\big|\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}\big|+\frac{2x}{\sigma_{n}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big|\widetilde{W_{i}}\{\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}-\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{(i)}\}\big|.

(A.28)

Furthermore, it follows from the distribution of
(\xi_{i},\eta_{i})
defined in (
A.7
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big|\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}\big|

\displaystyle=\exp\{-m(\lambda_{1})\}\mathbb{E}\left\{|\Delta_{n}|e^{\lambda_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\right\}

(A.29)

\displaystyle\leq 2x\exp\{-m(\lambda_{1})\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(V_{n}^{2}-1\right)^{2}e^{\lambda_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\right\},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big|\widetilde{W_{i}}\{\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}-\widetilde{\Delta}_{n}^{(i)}\}\big|

(A.30)

\displaystyle=\exp\{-m(\lambda_{1})\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\big|W_{i}\{\Delta_{n}-\Delta_{n}^{(i)}\}\big|e^{\lambda_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2x\exp\{-m(\lambda_{1})\}\mathbb{E}\Big\{|W_{i}|\Big|(V_{n}^{2}-1)^{2}-\Big(\sum_{j\neq i}Z_{j}\Big)^{2}\Big|e^{\lambda_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\Big\}

\displaystyle=2x\exp\{-m(\lambda_{1})\}\mathbb{E}\Big\{|W_{i}|\Big|Z_{i}^{2}+2Z_{i}\sum_{j\neq i}Z_{j}\Big|e^{\lambda_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\Big\}.

The lemma below presents the bounds of (
A.29
) and (
A.30
) and the proof will be presented in Section
B.3
.

Lemma A.4
.

For
x
satisfying (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) and
1/4<\lambda_{1}<3/4
, there exists an absolute constant
A
such that for
x>3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(V_{n}^{2}-1\right)^{2}e^{\lambda_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq A_{1}\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})\}x^{-2}R_{x}e^{A_{2}R_{x}},

(A.31)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big|W_{i}\big|\Big|Z_{i}^{2}+2Z_{i}\sum_{j\neq i}Z_{j}\Big|e^{\lambda_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq A_{1}\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})\}x^{-2}R_{x}e^{A_{2}R_{x}}.

(A.32)

In general, assume
0<r<r_{0}<1
for a constant
r_{0}
. For a number
\omega>r_{0}
, there exist constants
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
depending on
r_{0}
and
\omega
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big(V_{n}^{2}-1\big)^{2}e^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2rxX_{i}-\omega rx^{2}Y_{i}^{2})}\Big\}

(A.33)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A_{1}R_{x}}{x^{2}}\exp\Big\{(2r^{2}-\omega r)x^{2}-2\omega r^{2}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}+\frac{4}{3}r^{3}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}+A_{2}R_{x}\Big\}.

Consequently, by assembling (
A.27
)–(
A.32
) and (
A.12
)–(
A.14
), it holds that

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle\leq A\exp\{AR_{x}\}\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})-\lambda_{1}m_{n}\}(L_{3,n}+x^{-1}R_{x}).

(A.34)

Moreover, by observing that
m_{n}=m^{\prime}(\lambda_{1})=x^{2}+2xc
and
1-\Phi(2\lambda_{1}x)\geq Cx^{-1}e^{-2\lambda_{1}^{2}x^{2}}
for
x>2
and
1/4<\lambda_{1}<3/4
, we obtain

\displaystyle P_{2}

\displaystyle\leq A\exp\{AR_{x}\}\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})+(2\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{1})x^{2}-2\lambda_{1}xc\}

(A.35)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[1-\Phi(2\lambda_{1}x)\right]\{A_{4}xL_{3,n}+A_{5}R_{x}\},

which combined with (
A.26
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(2xS_{n}-x^{2}V_{n}^{2}\geq x^{2}+2xc-x\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{m(\lambda_{1})+(2\lambda_{1}^{2}-\lambda_{1})x^{2}-2\lambda_{1}xc\}\left[1-\Phi(2\lambda_{1}x)\right]e^{AR_{x}}(1+AxL_{3,n})

\displaystyle=\exp\big\{m(\lambda_{1})+\frac{1}{2}(x+c)^{2}-\lambda_{1}(x^{2}+2xc)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]e^{A_{3}R_{x}}(1+A_{4}xL_{3,n}),

where the last equality is derived by a similar procedure to (
A.23
). Finally, we arrive at the desired result (
5.7
) by using (
A.24
). The proof is completed.

A.4
Proof of Proposition
5.4

We use the truncation technique to estimate the error term. Let
B=2500\lor 200c_{0}
. Here the constants
2500
and
200
are set large enough for simplicity of proof, because we are not pursuing the best possible constants. The integrating region can be partitioned into three parts as follows,

\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}>xV_{n}+c,|V_{n}^{2}-1|>x^{-1}(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})\Big)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathbb{P}\left((S_{n},V_{n}^{2})\in\Omega_{i}\right),

(A.36)

where
\{\Omega_{i},i=1,2,3\}
are given by

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}

\displaystyle=\big\{(u,v)\in(R,R^{+}):~u>x\sqrt{v}+c,~1+x^{-1}(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})<v\leq B\big\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}

\displaystyle=\big\{(u,v)\in(R,R^{+}):~u>x\sqrt{v}+c,~v<1-x^{-1}(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})\big\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{3}

\displaystyle=\big\{(u,v)\in(R,R^{+}):~u>x\sqrt{v}+c,~v>B\big\}.

For the first part, we choose

r_{1}=x+c,\quad t_{1}=\frac{2}{5}x(x+c).

By Chebyshev’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big(S_{n},V_{n}^{2}\big)\in\Omega_{1}\big)

(A.37)

\displaystyle\leq x^{2}\exp\Big\{-\inf_{(u,v)\in\Omega_{1}}(r_{1}u-t_{1}v)\Big\}\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(V_{n}^{2}-1\right)^{2}e^{r_{1}S_{n}-t_{1}V_{n}^{2}}\right\}.

Obviously,

\displaystyle\inf_{(u,v)\in\Omega_{1}}(r_{1}u-t_{1}v)

(A.38)

\displaystyle=r_{1}\Big(c+x\sqrt{1+x^{-1}(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})}\Big)-t_{1}\big(1+x^{-1}(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})\big).

It follows from applying (
A.33
) in Lemma
A.4
with
r=r_{1}/(2x)
and
t=t_{1}/x^{2}
(It is easy to verify the conditions for (
A.33
) are satisfied when
|c|\leq x/5
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\left(V_{n}^{2}-1\right)^{2}e^{r_{1}S_{n}-t_{1}V_{n}^{2}}\}

(A.39)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{A_{1}R_{x}}{x^{2}}\exp\Big\{\frac{r_{1}^{2}}{2}-t_{1}+\frac{r_{1}^{3}}{6}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}-r_{1}t_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}]+A_{2}R_{x}\Big\}.

By plugging (
A.38
), (
A.39
) and the value of
r_{1},~t_{1}
into (
A.37
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}((S_{n},V_{n}^{2})\in\Omega_{1})

(A.40)

\displaystyle\leq A_{1}R_{x}\Psi_{x}^{*}\exp\Big\{-\frac{(x+c)^{2}}{2}+\frac{2}{5}\gamma^{2}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad-2x\gamma(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})\Big[\big(\sqrt{1+x^{-1}(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})}+1\big)^{-1}-\frac{2}{5}\Big]+A_{2}R_{x}\Big\},

where
\gamma=(x+c)/(2x)
. For
x>3
satisfying (
2.6
) with a small constant
c_{1}\leq\frac{1}{324}
, it holds that
x^{-1}(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})\leq 1/3
. Note also that
x^{3}|\mathbb{E}[X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}]|\leq\frac{2}{3}x^{3}L_{3,n}\leq xR_{x}^{1/2}
by Lemma
A.2
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}((S_{n},V_{n}^{2})\in\Omega_{1})

\displaystyle\leq A_{3}xR_{x}\Psi_{x}^{*}\big[1-\Phi(x+c)\big]\exp\Big\{0.4\gamma^{2}xR_{x}^{1/2}-2x\gamma(1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2})\cdot 0.06+A_{2}R_{x}\Big\}.

Furthermore, observe that
1\lor 6R_{x}^{1/2}\geq 1/2+3R_{x}^{1/2}
and
2/5\leq\gamma\leq 3/5
, therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}((S_{n},V_{n}^{2})\in\Omega_{1})

(A.41)

\displaystyle\leq A_{3}xR_{x}\Psi_{x}^{*}\big[1-\Phi(x+c)\big]\exp\{-0.024x-0.048xR_{x}^{1/2}+A_{2}R_{x}\}

\displaystyle\leq A_{4}R_{x}\Psi_{x}^{*}\big[1-\Phi(x+c)\big]e^{A_{2}R_{x}}.

As for the second error term, by choosing
r_{2}=x+c
and
t_{2}=2x(x+c)
, we have under (
2.6
) that

\inf_{(u,v)\in\Omega_{2}}(r_{2}u-t_{2}v)=r_{2}\Big(c+x\sqrt{1-x^{-1}(1\lor R_{x}^{1/2})}\Big)-t_{2}\Big(1-x^{-1}(1\lor R_{x}^{1/2})\Big).

(A.42)

In the same manner as the proof above, combining (
A.37
), (
A.41
) and (
A.42
) with
r_{1},t_{1}
replaced by
r_{2},t_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left(S_{n},V_{n}^{2}\right)\in\Omega_{2}\right)

(A.43)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq A_{1}xR_{x}\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]\Psi_{x}^{*}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\exp\Big\{-\frac{x}{2}\lor 3xR_{x}^{1/2}-6\gamma^{2}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}+A_{2}R_{x}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq A_{1}R_{x}[1-\Phi(x+c)]\Psi_{x}^{*}e^{A_{2}R_{x}}.

Next we deal with
\mathbb{P}\left(\left(S_{n},V_{n}^{2}\right)\in\Omega_{3}\right)
. Recall the notations

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{i}=X_{i}\mathbbm{1}(|(1+x)X_{i}|\leq 1),\quad\hat{S}_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{X}_{i},

\displaystyle\hat{Y}_{i}=Y_{i}\mathbbm{1}(|(1+x)Y_{i}|\leq 1),\quad{\hat{V}_{n}}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{Y}_{i}^{2}.

We also denote

\displaystyle\bar{X}_{i}=X_{i}\mathbbm{1}(|(1+x)X_{i}|>1),\quad\bar{S}_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{X}_{i},

\displaystyle\bar{Y}_{i}=Y_{i}\mathbbm{1}(|(1+x)Y_{i}|>1),\quad{\bar{V}_{n}}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{Y}_{i}^{2}.

It is evident that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left(S_{n},V_{n}^{2}\right)\in\Omega_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{S}_{n}>\frac{xV_{n}+c}{10},V_{n}>\sqrt{B}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\bar{S}_{n}>\frac{9\left(xV_{n}+c\right)}{10},V_{n}>\sqrt{B}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3},

where

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{S}_{n}>\frac{\sqrt{B}}{10}x+\frac{c}{10},\quad\hat{V}_{n}^{2}>\frac{B}{2}\right),

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{S}_{n}>\frac{\sqrt{B}}{10}x+\frac{c}{10},\quad\bar{V}_{n}^{2}>\frac{B}{2}\right),

\displaystyle K_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bar{S}_{n}>\frac{9\left(xV_{n}+c\right)}{10},V_{n}>\sqrt{B}\right).

By Chebyshev’s inequality and recalling that
B=\max\{2500,200c_{0}\}
we have

K_{1}\leq\frac{1}{\left(\frac{B}{2}-1\right)^{2}}\exp\left\{-\frac{3}{2}x^{2}-\frac{1}{20}xc\right\}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{V}_{n}^{2}-1\right)^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right].

(A.44)

Let
\hat{Z}_{i}=\hat{Y}_{i}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\hat{Y}_{i}^{2}
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{V}_{n}^{2}-1\right)^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right]

(A.45)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{Z}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bar{Y}_{i}^{2}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{Z}_{i}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bar{Y}_{i}^{2}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{Z}_{i}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[x^{-4}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}x^{3}\bar{Y}_{i}^{3}\right)^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}\hat{Z}_{i}^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{i}}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{i}}{2}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}+2x^{-4}R_{x}^{2}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+4\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\mathbb{E}\hat{Z}_{i}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{i}}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{i}}{2}}}\frac{\mathbb{E}\hat{Z}_{j}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}.

Furthermore, by Taylor expansion and the same argument used in Lemma
A.1
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big\{\frac{1}{8}x^{2}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{48}x^{3}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}+O_{1}\delta_{x,i}\Big\},

(A.46)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\hat{Z}_{i}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}

\displaystyle=O\left(x\left(\mathbb{E}|X_{i}|^{3}+\mathbb{E}|Y_{i}|^{3}\right)\right)+O\left(x^{-2}\delta_{x,i}\right),

(A.47)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\hat{Z}_{i}^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}

\displaystyle=O\left(x^{-4}\delta_{x,i}\right),\quad{\rm and}\quad\mathbb{E}\bar{Y}_{i}^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}=O\left(x^{-2}\delta_{x,i}\right).

(A.48)

By plugging (
A.46
)
-
(
A.48
) into (
A.45
), and the fact that
x^{4}L_{3,n}^{2}\leq 4\delta_{x}
, it is readily seen that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{V}_{n}^{2}-1\right)^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right]\leq\frac{AR_{x}}{x^{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{1}{8}x^{2}+\frac{1}{48}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}+A_{1}R_{x}\right\}.

(A.49)

Substituting (
A.49
) into (
A.44
) yields for
|c|\leq x/5
,

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}x^{-2}R_{x}\exp\Big\{-\frac{11}{8}x^{2}-\frac{xc}{20}+\frac{1}{48}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}+A_{1}R_{x}\Big\}

(A.50)

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}x^{-2}R_{x}\exp\left\{-\frac{(x+c)^{2}}{2}-\frac{1}{4}x^{2}+A_{1}R_{x}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]R_{x}\exp\left\{-\frac{1}{8}x^{2}+A_{1}R_{x}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}R_{x}\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]\Psi_{x}^{*}e^{A_{1}R_{x}},

and the last inequality holds because
x
satisfies (
2.6
) and

\displaystyle\big|x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}\big|\leq x^{3}L_{3,n},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\big|x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}Y_{i}^{2}\big|\leq x^{3}L_{3,n}.

Similarly, exploiting the upper bounds (
A.46
) and (
A.48
), we have

K_{2}<\frac{2}{B}\exp\left\{-\frac{3}{2}x^{2}-\frac{xc}{20}\right\}\mathbb{E}\left(\bar{V}_{n}^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right),

(A.51)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\bar{V}_{n}^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{S}_{n}}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{E}\Big(\bar{Y}_{i}^{2}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{i}}{2}}\Big)}{\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{i}}{2}}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{x\hat{X}_{j}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{AR_{x}}{x^{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{1}{8}x^{2}+\frac{1}{48}x^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}+A_{1}R_{x}\right\}.

Hence, in the same manner as the proof of (
A.50
), it follows that

K_{2}\leq AR_{x}\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]\Psi_{x}^{*}e^{A_{1}R_{x}}.

(A.52)

Finally, as for the bound for
K_{3}
, denote

\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}=\bar{X}_{i}\mathbbm{1}\big(2xX_{i}\leq\frac{X_{i}^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}\big):=\bar{X}_{i}\mathbbm{1}(\mathcal{M}),\quad{\rm and}\quad\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)}=\bar{X}_{i}-\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}.

Cauchy inequality leads to

\displaystyle K_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{X}_{i}>\frac{9\left(xV_{n}+c\right)}{10},\;\;V_{n}>\sqrt{B}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}>\frac{\left(xV_{n}+c\right)}{100},\;\;V_{n}>\sqrt{B}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)}>\frac{89\left(xV_{n}+c\right)}{100},\;\;V_{n}>\sqrt{B}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}>\frac{\left(x^{2}V_{n}+cx\right)}{50},\;\;V_{n}>\sqrt{B}\Big),

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)})^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}}>\frac{89}{100}\frac{xV_{n}+c}{\sqrt{V_{n}^{2}+c_{0}}},\;\;V_{n}>\sqrt{B}\Bigg)

\displaystyle:=K_{4}+K_{5}.

Recalling that
B=\max\{2500,200c_{0}\}
. we further have for
|c|\leq x/5

\displaystyle K_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}>0.996x^{2}\right)

(A.53)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}x^{-2}}{\exp\{0.986x^{2}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}e^{0.99\sum_{i=1}^{n}2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}x^{-2}}{\exp\{0.986x^{2}\}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}}\mathbbm{1}\big(|(1+x)X_{i}|>1,\mathcal{M}\big)\Big]\prod_{j\neq i}\mathbb{E}e^{2x\bar{X}_{j}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\leq AR_{x}\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]\Psi_{x}^{*}\exp{\{R_{x}\}},

\displaystyle K_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)})^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}>(0.884x)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}x^{-2}}{\exp\{0.773x^{2}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)})^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}e^{0.99\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)})^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{2}x^{-2}}{\exp\{0.773x^{2}\}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{(\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)})^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}}\mathbbm{1}\big(|x^{\prime}X_{i}|>1,\mathcal{M}^{c}\big)\Big]\prod_{j\neq i}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{(\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)})^{2}}{Y_{j}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{j}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq AR_{x}\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]\Psi_{x}^{*}\exp{\{R_{x}\}},

where we have used the condition
xL_{3,n}\leq c_{1}
, for
c_{1}
being some small enough constant, and that

\displaystyle\prod_{j\neq i}\mathbb{E}e^{2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j\neq i}\left\{1+\mathbb{E}e^{2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}}\mathbbm{1}(|x^{\prime}X_{i}|>1,\mathcal{M})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}e^{2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}}\mathbbm{1}(|x^{\prime}X_{i}|>1,\mathcal{M})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{r_{x}\}\leq\exp\{R_{x}\},

\displaystyle\prod_{j\neq i}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{(\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)})^{2}}{Y_{j}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{j}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j\neq i}\left\{1+\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{X_{i}^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}}\mathbbm{1}(|x^{\prime}X_{i}|>1,\mathcal{M}^{c})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}e^{\frac{X_{i}^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}}\mathbbm{1}(|x^{\prime}X_{i}|>1,\mathcal{M}^{c})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\{r_{x}\}\leq\exp\{R_{x}\},

\displaystyle R_{x}\geq r_{x}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{2x\bar{X}_{i}^{(1)}}\mathbbm{1}\big(|x^{\prime}X_{i}|>1,\mathcal{M}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{(\bar{X}_{i}^{(2)})^{2}}{Y_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}}}\mathbbm{1}\big(|x^{\prime}X_{i}|>1,\mathcal{M}^{c}\big)\Big].

Therefore by (
A.50
), (
A.52
) and (
A.53
), we conclude

\mathbb{P}\left(\left(S_{n},V_{n}^{2}\right)\in\Omega_{3}\right)\leq AR_{x}\left[1-\Phi(x+c)\right]\Psi_{x}^{*}e^{A_{1}R_{x}}.

(A.54)

Consequently, the desired bound (
5.8
) follows from (
A.40
), (
A.43
) and (
A.54
). The proof is completed.

A.5
Proof of Proposition
5.5

The main idea is to apply the general Theorem
2.1
. Recall the notation in (
5.11
), based on the one-dependent structure,
\{(X_{j},Y_{j})\}_{j=1}^{k}
is a sequence of independent random vectors. Further, the moment condition in Theorem
3.1
indicates

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}S_{n1}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}V_{n1}^{2}}-1\Big)=\rho_{n}+O\big(\frac{a^{2}}{n^{\alpha}}\big).

Hence

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big(S_{n1}\geq xV_{n1}+d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}xB_{n}\big)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{S_{n1}}{(\mathbb{E}S_{n1}^{2})^{1/2}}>\frac{x\big(1+O(a^{2}n^{-\alpha})\big)}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\frac{V_{n1}}{(\mathbb{E}V_{n1}^{2})^{1/2}}+O\big(d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}x\big)\right).\end{split}

(A.55)

We now focus on bounding the error terms involved in Theorem
2.1
. Note that
\{\xi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
are one-dependent sequence of random variables, and Rosenthal’s inequality yields for
2<p\leq 4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|X_{i}|^{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{j\in H_{i},odd}\xi_{j}+\sum_{j\in H_{i},even}\xi_{j}\Big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{j\in H_{i},odd}\xi_{j}\Big|^{p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{j\in H_{i},even}\xi_{j}\Big|^{p}\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq A\Bigg[\Big(\sum_{j\in H_{i},odd}\mathbb{E}\xi_{j}^{2}\Big)^{p/2}+\Big(\sum_{j\in H_{i},even}\mathbb{E}\xi_{j}^{2}\Big)^{p/2}+\sum_{j\in H_{i}}\mathbb{E}|\xi_{j}|^{p}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq Aa_{1}^{p}n^{p\alpha/2}.

In addition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|Y_{i}|^{p}\leq\big(\mathbb{E}|Y_{i}|^{4}\big)^{\frac{p}{4}}\leq\Big[k\sum_{j\in H_{i}}\mathbb{E}\xi_{j}^{4}\Big]^{\frac{p}{4}}\leq Aa_{1}^{p}n^{\frac{p\alpha}{2}},\quad B_{n1}^{2}:=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}Y_{i}^{2}\geq Aa_{2}^{2}n,

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}X_{j}Y_{j}^{2}\Big|+\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}X_{i}^{3}\Big|\leq 7\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j\in H_{i}}\big|\mathbb{E}\xi_{j}^{3}\big|\leq Aa_{1}^{3}n.

Hence

\displaystyle L_{3,n}\leq Aa^{3}n^{-\frac{1-\alpha}{2}},\quad\delta_{x}\leq Aa^{4}\frac{(1+x)^{4}}{n^{(1-\alpha)}},\quad\Psi_{x}^{*}\leq\exp\big(Aa^{3}x^{3}n^{-1/2}\big).

Thefore, applying Theorem
2.1
to (
A.55
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(S_{n1}\geq xV_{n1}+d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}xB_{n}\big)

(A.56)

\displaystyle=\Big[1-\Phi(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}})\Big]e^{O_{1}\sum r_{x,j}}\Big(1+O_{2}\big(\frac{a^{4}x^{4}}{n^{1-\alpha}}+\frac{a^{2}x^{2}}{n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}+\frac{a^{3}x}{n^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}}\big)\Big)

for
x\in(2,a^{-1}\min\{n^{\alpha/4},n^{(1-\alpha)/4}\})
, where

r_{x,j}\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\bigg\{\frac{(\sum_{i\in H_{j}}\xi_{i})^{2}}{\sum_{i\in H_{j}}(\xi_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2})}\bigg\}\mathbbm{1}\Big(x\Big|\sum_{i\in H_{j}}\xi_{i}\Big|>c(\rho)a_{2}n^{1/2}\Big)\bigg],

for some pre-assigned constant
c_{0}>0
and constant
c(\rho)
. The derivation of the upper bound of
r_{x,j}
is rather complicated. We exploit a technical lemma
A.5
whose proof will be provided in Section
B.4
to bound
r_{x,j}
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\{X_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
be a sequence of independent random variables with
\mathbb{E}X_{i}=0
and
\mathbb{E}|X_{i}|^{p}<\infty
for
p>2
. Denote
S_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
,
V_{n}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}^{2}
and
B_{n}^{2}=\mathbb{E}V_{n}^{2}
. Assume
b>0,A>0
. Then there exist positive constants
c_{0}
and
K
depending on
p
and
A
, such that

\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{AS_{n}^{2}}{V_{n}^{2}+c_{0}B_{n}^{2}}}\mathbbm{1}(b|S_{n}|>1)\Big]\leq Kb^{p}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|X_{i}|^{p}+(\mathbb{E}S_{n}^{2})^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big).

(A.57)

Since
\{\xi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
are one-dependent and Lemma
A.5
is built for independent random variables, we separate the odd-indexed terms and the even-indexed terms to apply Lemma
A.5
. Specifically,

\displaystyle r_{x,j}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{4|\sum_{i\in H_{j},odd}\xi_{i}|^{2}}{\sum_{i\in H_{j},odd}(\xi_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2})}\Big\}\mathbbm{1}\Big(\Big|\sum_{i\in H_{j},odd}\xi_{i}\Big|>\frac{c(\rho)a_{2}n^{1/2}}{2x}\Big)\bigg]

(A.58)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{4|\sum_{i\in H_{j},even}\xi_{i}|^{2}}{\sum_{i\in H_{j},even}(\xi_{i}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2})}\Big\}\mathbbm{1}\Big(\Big|\sum_{i\in H_{j},even}\xi_{i}\Big|>\frac{c(\rho)a_{2}n^{1/2}}{2x}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho)\frac{x^{4}}{a_{2}^{4}n^{2}}\Big[\sum_{i\in H_{j}}\mathbb{E}|\xi_{i}|^{4}+(\sum_{i\in H_{j},odd}\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2})^{2}+(\sum_{i\in H_{j},even}\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2})^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(\rho)\frac{a^{4}x^{4}}{n^{2-2\alpha}},

where the second inequality is derived by applying Lemma
A.5
. Therefore,
r_{x}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}r_{x,j}\leq C(\rho)a^{4}x^{4}/n^{1-\alpha}
, which together with (
A.56
) yields the desired result (
5.17
).

In the same manner as the proof of (
A.40
), using Chebyshev’s inequality and Taylor expansion, it is easy to obtain (
5.18
). To avoid redundancy, here we omit the proof.

A.6
Proof of Proposition
5.6

We start with the error bound (
5.19
). Denote
\omega_{j}=\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}-\mathbb{E}\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}
for
1\leq j\leq k
. Therefore,
\{\omega_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq k}
are independent random variables with
\omega_{j}\leq 2\tau
and
\mathbb{E}\omega_{j}=0
for each
j
. Recalling the definitions in (
5.12
) and
\tau=B_{n2}/x
.
For
d_{1}=\kappa_{\rho}a^{2}
with
\kappa_{\rho}\geq 10
, routine calculation shows

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\omega_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B_{n2}^{2}\leq a_{1}^{2}n^{1-\alpha},\quad d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}xB_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}\geq(\kappa_{\rho}-1)a_{1}xn^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}.

Hence, it follows from Bernstein inequality (see for example, Theorem A in
Fan, Grama and Liu 2015
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{S}_{n2}>d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}xB_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\omega_{j}\geq(\kappa_{\rho}-1)a_{1}xn^{(1-\alpha)/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(

\displaystyle-\frac{\kappa_{\rho}^{2}a_{1}^{2}x^{2}n^{1-\alpha}}{3B_{n2}^{2}+3\tau\kappa_{\rho}a_{1}xn^{\frac{(1-\alpha)}{2}}}\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\kappa_{\rho}x^{2}}{6}\Big).

As for inequality (
5.20
), similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n2}<-d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}xB_{n}+x(\hat{V}_{n}-V_{n1}),~V_{n1}^{2}>\frac{1}{4}B_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}<-d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}xB_{n}+x\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{k}\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}^{2}}{\hat{V}_{n}+V_{n1}},V_{n1}^{2}>\frac{1}{4}B_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\big(\gamma_{j}-\mathbb{E}\gamma_{j}\big)>d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}xB_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\gamma_{j}\Big),

where
\gamma_{j}=\frac{x}{B_{n}}\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}^{2}-\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}
,
1\leq j\leq k
, are independent random variables with
\gamma_{j}-\mathbb{E}\gamma_{j}\leq 4\tau
for each
j
. Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}(\gamma_{j}-\mathbb{E}\gamma_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\gamma_{j}^{2}\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{x^{2}\tau^{2}}{B_{n}^{2}}+1\Big)\mathbb{E}\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}^{2}\leq 4a_{1}^{2}n^{1-\alpha},

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\gamma_{j}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{x}{B_{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\hat{\xi}_{j(l+1)}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\xi_{j(l+1)}\mathbbm{1}(|\xi_{j(l+1)}|>\tau)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tau}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\xi_{j(l+1)}^{2}=xB_{n2}.

Again, Bernstein inequality leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n2}<-d_{1}n^{-\alpha/2}xB_{n}+x(\hat{V}_{n}-V_{n1}),~V_{n1}^{2}>\frac{1}{4}B_{n}^{2}\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\kappa_{\rho}x^{2}}{14}\Big).

The proof is completed.

A.7
Proof of Proposition
5.7

Observe that for any
a,b,\delta_{2}\in\mathbbm{R}
satisfying
3b\geq a^{2}
and
\delta_{2}\geq 0
, we have for arbitrary
x^{2}\geq 3
,

\displaystyle x\sqrt{b+\delta_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{x^{2}\Big(\frac{a^{2}}{3}+\delta_{2}\Big)}=\sqrt{(x^{2}-3)\frac{a^{2}}{3}+(x^{2}-3)\delta_{2}+a^{2}+3\delta_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{(x^{2}-3)\delta_{2}+a^{2}+2a\sqrt{\delta_{2}(x^{2}-3)}}\geq a+\sqrt{(x^{2}-3)\delta_{2}}.

Denote
H=\{j(\ell+1):1\leq j\leq k\}
, the index set of small block terms. We set

\displaystyle a:=\hat{\xi}_{j(\ell+1)}+\xi_{j(\ell+1)-1}+\xi_{j(\ell+1)+1},\quad\hat{S}_{n}^{(j)}:=\hat{S}_{n}-a,

\displaystyle b:=\hat{\xi}_{j(\ell+1)}^{2}+\xi_{j(\ell+1)-1}^{2}+\xi_{j(\ell+1)+1}^{2},\quad\delta_{2}:=\big(\hat{V}_{n}^{(j)}\big)^{2}=\hat{V}_{n}^{2}-b,

then
\{\hat{S}_{n}>x\hat{V}_{n}\}\subset\{\hat{S}_{n}^{(j)}>(\sqrt{x^{2}-3})\hat{V}_{n}^{(j)}\}
by the above analysis. Moreover,
\hat{S}_{n}^{(j)}
and
\hat{V}_{n}^{(j)}
are independent of
\xi_{j(l+1)}
since
\{\xi_{i},1\leq i\leq n\}
are one-dependent random variables. Thus it follows from (
5.22
) that for
x\in(c_{\rho}\sqrt{\log n},d_{0}a^{-1}n^{1/8})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n}\geq x\hat{V}_{n},\max_{1\leq j\leq k}|\xi_{j(l+1)}|>\tau\Big)

(A.59)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in H}\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n}^{(i)}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3}\hat{V}_{n}^{(i)}\Big)\mathbb{P}\big(|\xi_{i}|>\tau\big)

\displaystyle\leq A\frac{\sum_{i\in H}\mathbb{E}|\xi_{i}|^{4}}{\tau^{4}}\Bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{\sqrt{x^{2}-3}}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}^{(i)}}}\Big)\Bigg]\Big(1+O(\frac{a^{4}x^{2}}{n^{1/4}})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq Aa^{4}\frac{(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\Bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{\sqrt{x^{2}-3}}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\Big)\Bigg]\Big(1+O(\frac{a^{4}x^{2}}{n^{1/4}})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq Ae^{3/2(1-2\rho)}a^{4}\frac{(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\Bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\Big)\Bigg]\Big(1+O(\frac{a^{4}x^{2}}{n^{1/4}})\Big).

where
\rho_{n}^{(i)}
is defined parallel to
\rho_{n}
and

\displaystyle\rho_{n}^{(i)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(\hat{S}_{n}^{(i)})^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[(\hat{V}_{n}^{(i)})^{2}]}-1\Big)=\rho_{n}\Big(1+O\big(\frac{a^{2}}{n}\big)\Big)

because
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{4}\leq a_{1}^{4}
and
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2}\geq a_{2}^{2}
for
i\geq 1
. Then (
5.23
) is derived.

Regarding the proof of (
5.24
), a more technical iterative argument will be exploited. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}\geq xV_{n},\max_{1\leq j\leq k}|\xi_{j(l+1)}|>\tau\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\in H}\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}^{(i_{1})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3}V_{n}^{(i_{1})}\Big)\mathbb{P}\big(|\xi_{i_{1}}|>\tau\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\in H}A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n}\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}^{(i_{1})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3}V_{n}^{(i_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\in H}A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big[\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n}^{(i_{1})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3}\hat{V}_{n}^{(i_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}^{(i_{1})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3}V_{n}^{(i_{1})},\max_{j\in H\setminus\{i_{1}\}}|\xi_{j(l+1)}|>\tau\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\in H}A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big[\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n}^{(i_{1})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3}\hat{V}_{n}^{(i_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{i_{2}\in H\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}^{(i_{1},i_{2})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-6}V_{n}^{(i_{1},i_{2})}\Big)\mathbb{P}\big(|\xi_{i_{2}}|>\tau\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\in H}A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n}\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n}^{(i_{1})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3}\hat{V}_{n}^{(i_{1})}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i_{1}\in H}\sum_{i_{2}\in H\setminus\{i_{1}\}}\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}^{(i_{1},i_{2})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-6}V_{n}^{(i_{1},i_{2})}\Big).

(A.60)

Put
u=[\frac{x^{2}}{6}]
so that
\sqrt{x^{2}-3u}\approx x/\sqrt{2}
and we repeat the above procedure iteratively up to
u
times,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}\geq xV_{n},\max_{1\leq j\leq k}|\xi_{j(l+1)}|>\tau\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{u-1}\sum_{i_{1}\in H}\cdots\sum_{i_{j}\in H}\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big)^{j}\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n}^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{j})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3j}\hat{V}_{n}^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{j})}\Big)

\displaystyle~\quad+\sum_{i_{1}\in H}\cdots\sum_{i_{u}\in H}\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big)^{u}\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{u})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3u}V_{n}^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{u})}\Big).

As we have chosen
\alpha=1/2
and
|H|=k\asymp n^{1-\alpha}=\sqrt{n}
, it follows by (
5.22
) that for arbitrary
i_{1},\cdots,i_{j}\in H
, and
1\leq j\leq x^{2}/6
, we have for
x\in(c_{\rho}\sqrt{\log n},d_{0}a^{-2}n^{1/8})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}\in H}\cdots\sum_{i_{j}\in H}\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big)^{j}\mathbb{P}\Big(\hat{S}_{n}^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{j})}\geq\sqrt{x^{2}-3j}\hat{V}_{n}^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{j})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\Big)^{j}\bigg[1-\Phi\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{x^{2}-3j}}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}^{(i_{1},\cdots,i_{j})}}}\bigg)\bigg]\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{a^{4}x^{2}}{n^{1/4}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq A\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\Big)^{j}e^{3j/(2+4\rho_{n})}\bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\Big)\bigg]\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{a^{4}x^{2}}{n^{1/4}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}e^{3/(2+4\rho_{n})}\Big)^{j-1}\bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\cdot\big(\frac{1}{2}\big)^{j-1}\bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\Big)\bigg]

for sufficiently large
n
and some constant
A^{\prime\prime}
depending on
a,\rho
, where
\rho_{n}^{(i_{1},\cdots,i_{j})}
is defined as

\displaystyle\rho_{n}^{(i_{1},\cdots,i_{j})}:=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}[(\hat{S}_{n}^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{j})})^{2}]}{\mathbb{E}[(\hat{V}_{n}^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{j})})^{2}]}-1\Big)=\rho_{n}\Big(1+O\big(\frac{a^{2}x^{2}}{n}\big)\Big)

Therefore, by combining the upper bounds above we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}\geq xV_{n},\max_{1\leq j\leq k}|\xi_{j(l+1)}|>\tau\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{u-1}A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\cdot\big(\frac{1}{2}\big)^{j-1}\bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\Big)\bigg]+\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\Big)^{u}

\displaystyle\leq A_{1}\frac{a^{4}x^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\Big)\bigg]\Big(1+\Big(A\frac{a^{4}(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1/2}}e^{3/(1+2\rho_{n})}\Big)^{x^{2}/3}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq A_{2}\frac{a^{4}x^{4}}{n^{1/2}}\bigg[1-\Phi\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+2\rho_{n}}}\Big)\bigg]

for
x\in(c_{\rho}\sqrt{\log n},d_{0}a^{-2}n^{1/8})
. The proof is completed.

A.8
Proof of Proposition
5.8

We apply Theorem
2.1
to estimate
\mathbb{P}(S_{k,1}\geq xV_{k,1}-\varepsilon xB_{n})
. As a preparation, we first calculate the relevant moments. By Lemma
5.1
, there exist some positive constants
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
depending on
a_{1},a_{2}
and
\tau
such that for
1\leq u\leq k_{1}
,

\mathbb{E}\xi_{u}^{4}+\mathbb{E}\eta_{u}^{4}\leq A_{1}n^{2(\alpha+\alpha_{1})}\mu_{1}^{4},\quad\mathbb{E}|\xi_{u}|^{3}+\mathbb{E}|\eta_{u}|^{3}\leq A_{2}n^{\frac{3}{2}(\alpha+\alpha_{1})}\mu_{1}^{3}.

(A.61)

In addition, by Lemma
5.2
and some routine calculations we can obtain

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\xi_{u}^{3}|\leq A_{3}n^{(\alpha+\alpha_{1})}c_{1}^{3},\quad|\mathbb{E}\xi_{u}\eta_{u}^{2}|\leq A_{3}n^{(\alpha+\alpha_{1})}c_{1}^{3},

(A.62)

for a positive constant
A_{3}
depending on
a_{1},a_{2}
and
\tau
.
To apply the moderate deviation theorem for general self-normalized sums, it remains to estimate the error term

r_{x,u}:=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big(\frac{\xi_{u}^{2}}{\eta_{u}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}\eta_{u}^{2}}\Big)\mathbbm{1}\Big(\frac{1+x}{B_{n,1}}|\xi_{u}|>1\Big)\bigg].

We split the block
I_{u}
into odd-indexed terms and even-indexed terms to construct a weakly dependent structure so that Lemma
5.3
can be applied. To be specific, let
\mathcal{J}_{1}=\{j:j~\mbox{ is odd}\}
and
\mathcal{J}_{2}=\{j:j~\mbox{is even}\}
.
Correspondingly, we define for
t=1,2

\mathcal{G}_{t}:=\sum_{j\in I_{u}\cap\mathcal{J}_{t}}Y_{j},\quad\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2}:=\sum_{j\in I_{u}\cap\mathcal{J}_{t}}\big(Y_{j}^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}Y_{j}^{2}\big).

In the same manner as (
A.58
), we have

\displaystyle r_{x,u}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\frac{4\mathcal{G}_{t}^{2}}{\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2}}\Big)\mathbbm{1}\Big(\frac{(1+x)|\mathcal{G}_{t}|}{B_{n,1}}>\frac{1}{v}\Big)\Big]:=\sum_{t=1}^{2}r_{x,u,t}.

(A.63)

As
\{r_{x,u,t},t=1,2\}
share the same bound, we only present the analysis of
r_{x,u,1}
. Suppose the cardinality of
I_{u}\cap\mathcal{J}_{1}
is
[n^{\alpha_{1}}/2]
for simplicity. By Lemma
5.3
,
\{Y_{j},j\in I_{u}\cap\mathcal{J}_{1}\}
can be approximated by a sequence of independent random variables
\{Y_{j}^{*},1\leq j\leq[n^{\alpha_{1}}/2]\}
. Denote

\mathcal{G}_{1}^{*}=\sum_{j=1}^{[n^{\alpha_{1}}/2]}Y_{j}^{*},\quad(\mathcal{K}_{1}^{*})^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{[n^{\alpha_{1}}/2]}\big((Y_{j}^{*})^{2}+c_{0}\mathbb{E}(Y_{j}^{*})^{2}\big).

It follows from Lemmas
A.5
and
5.3
that

\displaystyle r_{x,u,1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\frac{4(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{*})^{2}}{(\mathcal{K}_{1}^{*})^{2}}\Big)\mathbbm{1}\Big(\frac{(1+x)|\mathcal{G}_{1}^{*}|}{B_{n,1}}>\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big]

(A.64)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\frac{4\mathcal{G}_{1}^{2}}{\mathcal{K}_{1}^{2}}\Big)\mathbbm{1}\Big(\frac{(1+x)|\mathcal{G}_{1}|}{B_{n,1}}>\frac{1}{2}\Big)\mathbbm{1}\big(\,\exists j:Y_{j}\neq Y_{j}^{*}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\frac{4(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{*})^{2}}{(\mathcal{K}_{1}^{*})^{2}}\Big)\mathbbm{1}\Big(\frac{(1+x)|\mathcal{G}_{1}^{*}|}{B_{n,1}}>\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big]+\frac{a_{1}n^{\alpha_{1}}}{2}e^{4n^{\alpha_{1}}-a_{2}n^{\alpha\tau}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(1+x)^{4}}{n^{2(1-\alpha-\alpha_{1})}}+\frac{a_{1}n^{\alpha_{1}}}{2}e^{4n^{\alpha_{1}}-a_{2}n^{\alpha\tau}},

where
\alpha_{1}
should be chosen such that
\alpha_{1}\leq\alpha\tau
. Therefore, by Theorem
2.1
and plugging in the bounds given in (
5.27
), (
A.61
), (
A.62
) and (
A.64
), we can obtain there exist a constant
c_{0}
depending on
d_{1},\mu_{1}/\mu_{2},a_{1},a_{2},\alpha
and
\tau
such that when
\epsilon=d_{1}n^{-\alpha_{1}/2}\log n
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(S_{k,1}\geq xV_{k,1}\pm\varepsilon xB_{n}\big)\big/\big[1-\Phi(x)\big]

(A.65)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+O\bigg(\frac{(1+x)^{4}}{n^{1-\alpha-\alpha_{1}}}+\frac{1+x}{n^{(1-\alpha-\alpha_{1})/2}}+\frac{(1+x)^{2}}{n^{\alpha}}+\frac{(1+x)^{2}\log n}{n^{\alpha_{1}/2}}\bigg)

uniformly for
x\in(0,c_{0}\min\{n^{(1-\alpha-\alpha_{1})/4},n^{\alpha\tau/2},n^{\alpha/2},(\log n)^{-1/2}n^{\alpha_{1}/4}\})
. The proof of Proposition
5.8
is completed.

A.9
Proof of Proposition
5.9

Note that
\lim_{\ell\rightarrow-\infty}\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|\mathscr{F}_{\ell})
belongs to the tail
\sigma
-field, hence
\lim_{\ell\rightarrow-\infty}\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|\mathscr{F}_{\ell})=\mathbb{E}(X_{i})=0
by the zero-one law. Therefore, we may write
X_{i}
as

X_{i}=\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{i-u}X_{i},\qquad{\rm where}~\ \mathcal{P}_{i-u}(\cdot)=\mathbb{E}(\cdot|\mathscr{F}_{i-u})-\mathbb{E}(\cdot|\mathscr{F}_{i-u-1}).

Observe that,

\parallel Y_{j}\parallel_{4}=\Big\|\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i\in H_{j}}\mathcal{P}_{i-u}X_{i}\Big\|_{4}\leq\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\Big\|\sum_{i\in H_{j}}\mathcal{P}_{i-u}X_{i}\Big\|_{4}.

For fixed
u\geq 0
, denote
Z_{i}=\mathcal{P}_{i-u}X_{i}
and
\mathscr{A}_{i}=\mathscr{F}_{i-u}
. It is obvious that
Z_{i}\in\mathscr{A}_{i}
and
\mathscr{A}_{i-1}\subset\mathscr{A}_{i}
for any
i\geq 1
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}|\mathscr{A}_{i-1})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|\mathscr{A}_{i-1})-\mathbb{E}(X_{i}|\mathscr{A}_{i-1})=0,

thus for any fixed
u\geq 0
,
\left\{(Z_{i},\mathscr{A}_{i}),i\geq 1\right\}
is a martingale difference sequence. It follows from Burkholder’s martingale inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{i\in H_{j}}Z_{i}\Big|^{4}\Big]\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{i\in H_{j}}Z_{i}^{2}\Big|^{2}\Big]\leq Cm\sum_{i\in H_{j}}\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{4}).

In addition, by conditional Jensen’s inequality and recalling the definition in (
3.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{i}^{4})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\mathbb{E}\big(X_{i}\big|\mathscr{F}_{i-u}\big)-\mathbb{E}\big(X_{i}\big|\mathscr{F}_{i-u-1}\big)\Big|^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big\{\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[X_{i}-G_{i}\big(\ldots,\varepsilon_{i-u-1},\epsilon_{i-u}^{\ast},\varepsilon_{i-u+1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{i}\big)\big|\mathscr{F}_{i-u}\big]\Big|^{4}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\big(X_{i}-G_{i}\big(\ldots,\varepsilon_{i-u-1},\epsilon_{i-u}^{\ast},\varepsilon_{i-u+1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{i}\big)\big)^{4}\big]=\big[\theta_{4}(u)\big]^{4}.

Thus, we obtain by condition (
3.16
) and Remark
3.2
that

\displaystyle\parallel Y_{j}\parallel_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\Big\|\sum_{i\in H_{j}}Z_{i}\Big\|_{4}\leq C\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}m^{1/2}a_{1}^{\prime}e^{-a_{2}^{\prime}u^{\tau}}\leq C_{1}m^{1/2}

for
C_{1}
being a constant depending on
a_{1},a_{2}
and
\tau
. Further, it follows from Minkowski’s inequality and (
5.38
) that for sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle\parallel\tilde{Y}_{j}\parallel_{4}\leq\parallel Y_{j}\parallel_{4}+\parallel\tilde{Y}_{j}-Y_{j}\parallel_{4}\leq C_{1}m^{1/2}+ma_{1}e^{-a_{2}m^{\tau}}\leq\tilde{C}_{1}m^{1/2},

(A.66)

\displaystyle\parallel\tilde{Y}_{j}\parallel_{2}\geq\parallel Y_{j}\parallel_{2}-\parallel\tilde{Y}_{j}-Y_{j}\parallel_{2}\geq c_{1}m^{1/2}-ma_{1}e^{-a_{2}m^{\tau}}\geq\tilde{C}_{2}m^{1/2},

(A.67)

where
\tilde{C}_{1}
and
\tilde{C}_{2}=\omega_{1}-n^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}a_{1}e^{-a_{2}n^{\alpha\tau}}>\omega_{1}/2
are constants depending on
\tau,\alpha,\omega_{1},a_{1}
and
a_{2}
.

Denote
\rho_{k}=\big(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{j}\tilde{Y}_{j+1})\big)\big/\big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{j}^{2})\big)
. Note that

\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}\cdot n=\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}\cdot km\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2})\leq\tilde{C}_{1}^{2}\cdot km=\tilde{C}_{1}^{2}\cdot n,

and

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}\Big(\tilde{Y}_{j}\tilde{Y}_{j+1}\Big)-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}\Big(Y_{j}Y_{j+1}\Big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[Y_{j}\Big(\tilde{Y}_{j+1}-Y_{j+1}\Big)\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[Y_{j+1}\Big(\tilde{Y}_{j}-Y_{j}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\tilde{Y}_{j}-Y_{j}\Big)\Big(\tilde{Y}_{j+1}-Y_{j+1}\Big)\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\Big(\big\|Y_{j}\big\|_{2}\cdot\big\|\tilde{Y}_{j+1}-Y_{j+1}\big\|_{2}+\big\|Y_{j+1}\big\|_{2}\cdot\big\|\tilde{Y}_{j}-Y_{j}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\big\|\tilde{Y}_{j}-Y_{j}\big\|_{2}\cdot\big\|\tilde{Y}_{j+1}-Y_{j+1}\big\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76228pt\leq k\left(2\tilde{C}_{1}m^{1/2}\cdot ma_{1}e^{-a_{2}m^{\tau}}+m^{2}a_{1}^{2}e^{-2a_{2}m^{\tau}}\right).

Immediately we have

\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i+1})-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}(Y_{i}Y_{i+1})\bigg|\bigg/\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2})\bigg)\leq C_{3}e^{-C_{4}a_{2}m^{\tau}},

(A.68)

with constants
C_{3}
and
C_{4}
depending on
\tau,\alpha,\omega_{1},a_{1}
and
a_{2}
. Now, for further investigation into the ratio of
\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}(Y_{j}Y_{j+1})
against
\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2})
, we make use of the martingale structure again by representing
Y_{j}
and
Y_{j+1}
as

Y_{j}=\sum_{i\in H_{j}}\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{i-u}X_{i},\qquad Y_{j+1}=\sum_{i\in H_{j+1}}\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{i-u}X_{i}.

Thus

\mathbb{E}(Y_{j}Y_{j+1})=\sum_{i_{1}\in H_{j}}\sum_{i_{2}\in H_{j+1}}\sum_{u_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{u_{2}=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathcal{P}_{i_{1}-u_{1}}X_{i_{1}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\mathcal{P}_{i_{2}-u_{2}}X_{i_{2}}\Big)\Big].

Note that
\mathcal{P}_{i_{2}-u_{2}}X_{i_{2}}\in\mathscr{F}_{i_{2}-u_{2}}
and for any
\ell_{1}<i_{1}-u_{1}
,
\ell_{2}<i_{2}-u_{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{P}_{i_{1}-u_{1}}X_{i_{1}}\big|\mathscr{F}_{\ell_{1}}\Big]=0,\quad\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{P}_{i_{2}-u_{2}}X_{i_{2}}\big|\mathscr{F}_{\ell_{2}}\Big]=0.

Therefore, for
i_{1}-u_{1}\neq i_{2}-u_{2}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathcal{P}_{i_{1}-u_{1}}X_{i_{1}}\right)\cdot\left(\mathcal{P}_{i_{2}-u_{2}}X_{i_{2}}\right)\right]=0
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}(Y_{j}Y_{j+1})\big|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum_{i_{1}\in H_{j}}\sum_{i_{2}\in H_{j+1}}\sum_{u_{1}=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathcal{P}_{i_{1}-u_{1}}X_{i_{1}}\Big)\cdot\Big(\mathcal{P}_{i_{1}-u_{1}}X_{i_{2}}\Big)\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\in H_{j}}\sum_{i_{2}\in H_{j+1}}\sum_{u_{1}=0}^{+\infty}4a_{1}^{\prime 2}\cdot\exp\Big[-a_{2}^{\prime}\Big(u_{1}^{\tau}+(i_{2}-i_{1}+u_{1})^{\tau}\Big)\Big],

where we have used the fact that
\|\mathcal{P}_{i_{1}-u_{1}}X_{i_{1}}\|_{2}\leq\theta_{4}(u_{1})
and
\|\mathcal{P}_{i_{1}-u_{1}}X_{i_{2}}\|_{2}\leq\theta_{4}(i_{2}-i_{1}+u_{1})
.

It is trivial to see from the integrability of the function
e^{-ax^{\tau}}
that

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}\in H_{j}}\sum_{i_{2}\in H_{j+1}}\sum_{u_{1}=0}^{\infty}4a_{1}^{\prime 2}\cdot\exp\Big[-a_{2}^{\prime}\Big(u_{1}^{\tau}+(i_{2}-i_{1}+u_{1})^{\tau}\Big)\Big]

(A.69)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=1}^{2m}\ell\cdot\exp\Big[-a_{2}^{\prime}\Big(u^{\tau}+(\ell+u)^{\tau}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{2m}\ell e^{-a_{2}^{\prime}\ell^{\tau}}\right)\left(\sum_{u=0}^{\infty}e^{-a_{2}^{\prime}u^{\tau}}\right)\leq C_{5},

for some constant
C_{5}
depending on
\alpha,a_{1},a_{2}
and
\tau
. Finally, as a result of (
A.67
), (
A.68
) and (
A.69
), we obtain

|\rho_{k}|:=\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{i}\tilde{Y}_{i+1})\bigg|\bigg/\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{i}^{2})\bigg)\leq C_{6}m^{-1}

(A.70)

for large enough
n
, where
C_{6}
is a constant depending on
\tau,\alpha,\omega_{1},a_{1}
and
a_{2}
.

Now we are ready to apply Theorem
3.1
to the self-normalized sum

\tilde{T}_{k}=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{k}\tilde{Y}_{j}}{(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\tilde{Y}_{j}^{2})^{1/2}}.

Notice that
\{\tilde{Y}_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq k}
are one-dependent random variables and the bounds in (
A.66
), (
A.67
) and (
A.70
), i.e., the conditions in Theorem
3.1
, are satisfied. Recall that
m=[n^{\alpha}]
and
k=[n^{1-\alpha}]
. We obtain by (
3.2
) that there is a constant
d_{0}
depending on
\tau,\alpha,\omega_{1},a_{1}
and
a_{2}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{T}_{k}\geq x)

\displaystyle=\left[1-\Phi\left(\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+O(m^{-1})}}\right)\right]\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1+x^{2}}{n^{(1-\alpha)/4}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big[1-\Phi(x)\Big]\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{1+x^{2}}{n^{(1-\alpha)/4}}+\frac{1+x^{2}}{n^{\alpha}}\Big)\Big),

uniformly for
x\in(0,d_{0}\min\{n^{(1-\alpha)/8},n^{\alpha/2}\})
. This completes the proof of Proposition
5.9
.

A.10
Proof of Proposition
5.10

For simplicity, we denote
V_{k}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}Y_{j}^{2}
and
\tilde{V}_{k}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\tilde{Y}_{j}^{2}
. Recall the constant
\tilde{C}_{2}
in the lower bound shown in (
A.67
). Set

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}=\left\{\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\big|Y_{i}-\tilde{Y}_{i}\big|\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}},~\tilde{V}_{k}^{2}\geq\frac{\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}}{2}\cdot n\right\}.

Within the set
\mathcal{B}
, it holds that

\displaystyle|V_{k}-\tilde{V}_{k}|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{V}_{k}^{-1}|V_{k}^{2}-\tilde{V}_{k}^{2}|\leq n^{-2}\tilde{V}_{k}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}(2|\tilde{Y}_{j}|+n^{-2})

\displaystyle\leq 2n^{-2}k^{1/2}+\sqrt{2}\tilde{C}_{2}^{-1}n^{-7/2-\alpha}\leq(2\tilde{C}_{2}^{-1}+2)n^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}},

hence when
n
is sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\big|T_{k}-\tilde{T}_{k}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{\Big|\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}Y_{j}\Big)\tilde{V_{k}}-\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\tilde{Y}_{j}\Big)V_{k}\Big|}{V_{k}\tilde{V}_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{k}\big|Y_{j}-\tilde{Y}_{j}\big|}{V_{k}}+\frac{\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\big|\tilde{Y}_{j}\big|\Big)}{\tilde{V}_{k}}\cdot\frac{\big|V_{k}-\tilde{V}_{k}\big|}{V_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(n^{-1/2}kn^{-2}+k^{1/2}n^{-1/2}n^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2Cn^{-\frac{3}{2}-\alpha}\leq 2Cn^{-1}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(T_{k}\geq x\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{k}\geq x,~\big|T_{k}-\tilde{T}_{k}\big|\leq 2Cn^{-1}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c})

(A.71)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\tilde{T}_{k}\geq x-2Cn^{-1}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c}),

and similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(T_{k}\geq x\big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\big(T_{k}\geq x,~\mathcal{B}\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\Big(\tilde{T}_{k}\geq x+2Cn^{-1},~\mathcal{B}\Big)

(A.72)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\Big(\tilde{T}_{k}\geq x+2Cn^{-1}\Big)-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c}).

As for the bound of
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c})
, notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}\big(\big|Y_{j}-\tilde{Y}_{j}\big|>n^{-2}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\tilde{V}_{k}^{2}\leq\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}n/2\big).

By Chebyshev’s inequality and (
5.38
) with
r=2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}\big(\big|Y_{j}-\tilde{Y}_{j}\big|>n^{-2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{1-\alpha}n^{4+2\alpha}a_{1}^{2}e^{-2a_{2}n^{\alpha\tau}}\leq Ce^{-a_{2}n^{\alpha\tau}}.

In addition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{V}_{k}^{2}\leq\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}n/2)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k/2}\tilde{Y}_{2\ell-1}^{2}<\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}n/4\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k/2}\tilde{Y}_{2\ell}^{2}<\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}n/4\Big),

where, without loss of generality, we assumed that
k/2
is an integer.
Denoting
W_{\ell}=-\tilde{Y}_{2\ell-1}^{2}+\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{2\ell-1}^{2})
,
1\leq\ell\leq k/2
, (
A.66
) suggests that

\displaystyle W_{\ell}\leq\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{2\ell-1}^{2})\leq\tilde{C}_{1}^{2}m,\quad\quad\mathbb{E}(W_{\ell})=0,

\displaystyle{\rm and}\quad\sum_{\ell=1}^{k/2}\mathbb{E}(W_{\ell}^{2})=\sum_{i=1}^{k/2}\var(\tilde{Y}_{2i-1}^{2})\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{k/2}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Y}_{2\ell-1}^{4})\leq\tilde{C}_{1}^{4}km^{2}/2.

Observe that
\{Y_{2\ell-1},1\leq\ell\leq k/2\}
is a sequence of independent random variables. It follows from Bernstein’s inequality and (
A.67
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k/2}

\displaystyle Y_{2\ell-1}^{2}\leq\frac{\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}}{4}n\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k/2}W_{\ell}\geq-\frac{\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}}{4}n+\sum_{i=1}^{k/2}\mathbb{E}\big(\tilde{Y}_{2i-1}^{2}\big)\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k/2}W_{\ell}\geq\frac{\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}}{4}n\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big\{-\frac{\big((\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}n)/4\big)^{2}}{2\big(\tilde{C}_{1}^{2}m\cdot\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}n/4+\tilde{C}_{1}^{4}km^{2}/2\big)}\Big\}\leq\exp\{-Cn^{1-\alpha}\},

for some constant
C
depends on
\tau,\alpha,\omega_{1},a_{1}
and
a_{2}
. By the same token,

\mathbb{P}\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k/2}Y_{2\ell}^{2}\leq\frac{\tilde{C}_{2}^{2}}{4}n\Big)\leq\exp\{-Cn^{1-\alpha}\}.

Overall, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c})\leq Ce^{-a_{2}n^{\alpha\tau}}+2e^{-Cn^{1-\alpha}}.

(A.73)

The proof of Proposition
5.10
is completed.

A.11
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Define
f(x)=x\mathbbm{1}\{|x|\leq\tau\}+\tau\mathbbm{1}\{x>\tau\}-\tau\mathbbm{1}\{x<-\tau\}
.
In view of the expression shown in (
4.10
) and the notations presented above Theorem
4.1
, the main idea is to apply Theorem
2.1
and estimate the corresponding bias-corrected term
\Psi_{x}^{*}
and error terms
L_{3,n}
and
R_{x}
under fourth moment, which are defined by

\displaystyle L_{3,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-\tilde{\mu}|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}^{3}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-{\mu}|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{2}^{3}}

\displaystyle\Psi_{x}^{*}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big\{(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}})^{3}x^{3}n^{-1/2}\Big(\frac{4}{3}\gamma^{3}\frac{\mathbb{E}(f(Y)-\tilde{\mu})^{3}}{\sigma_{1}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-2\gamma^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(f(Y)-\tilde{\mu})(f(Y)-{\mu})^{2}]}{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle R_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-\tilde{\mu}|^{4}}{\sigma_{1}^{4}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-{\mu}|^{4}}{\sigma_{2}^{4}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{(f(Y)-\tilde{\mu})^{2}}{(f(Y)-\mu)^{2}+\sigma_{2}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\Big\}\mathbbm{1}\big((1+x)\frac{|f(Y)-\tilde{\mu}|}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}}>1\big)\bigg],

where
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}(1+\frac{c}{x})
.

Throughout the rest of the proof,
A
is an absolute positive constant which may vary at each appearance. When the higher moment
\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}]<\infty
is assumed, we can figure out more accurate estimate for the bias-corrected term
\Psi_{x}^{*}
. Let us collect the bounds for some crucial quantities in the proposition below. The proof of Proposition
A.1
will be given in Section
A.16
.

Proposition A.1
.

If
\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}<\infty
, then we have there exists an absolute positive constant
A
such that

\displaystyle|\sigma_{1}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{\tau^{2}},\quad|\sigma_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{\tau^{2}},\quad|\mu-\tilde{\mu}|\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{\tau^{3}}.

(A.74)

Moreover, when
\tau
satisfies (
4.11
), we have

\Big|\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}\tau^{6}},

(A.75)

and there exist absolute positive constants
A
and
a_{0}
such that

\Big|\frac{1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x+c\big)}{1-\Phi(x)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(1+x)\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{\sigma\tau^{3}}

(A.76)

for
0<x\leq a_{0}\frac{\tau^{3}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}
, and

R_{x}\leq\frac{A(1+x)^{4}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{n\sigma^{4}}.

(A.77)

In addition, there exist positive constants
a_{1}
and
C
depending on
\sigma
,
\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}
and
\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}
such that

\Psi_{x}^{*}=\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{3}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mu)^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}\Big\}\Big[1+O_{1}\Big(\frac{x^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\tau}+\frac{x^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\Big)\Big]

(A.78)

for
2<x\leq a_{1}\min\{\frac{\tau^{3}}{\sqrt{n}},(\sqrt{n}\tau)^{1/3},\tau^{3/2}\}
, where
O_{1}
is a bounded quantity such that
|O_{1}|\leq C
.

We next consider two cases of
0<x\leq 2
and
x>2
, separately.
First for
0<x\leq 2
, it is immediate that
1-\Phi(x)\geq 1-\Phi(2)
. Moreover, it follows from Proposition
5.1
and (
A.76
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=[1-\Phi(x)]\Big[1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}\Big)\Big].

Since the bias term
\exp\{-\frac{x^{3}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mu)^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}\}=1+O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})
for
0<x\leq 2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=[1-\Phi(x)]\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{3}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mu)^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}\Big\}\Big[1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}\Big)\Big]

(A.79)

for
0<x\leq 2
, which completes the proof of (
4.12
) for
0<x\leq 2
. Then we deal with the case of
x>2
. Applying Theorem
2.1
to (
4.10
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=\Big[1-\Phi(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x+c)\Big]\Psi_{x}^{*}e^{O_{1}R_{x}}\Big(1+O_{2}(1+\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x)L_{3,n}\Big)

(A.80)

uniformly for
|c|\leq x/5
and
x>2
satisfying (
2.6
) and (
2.7
). Note that (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) are satisfied when
2<x\leq a_{2}\frac{\sqrt{n}\sigma^{4}}{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}
for some constant
a_{2}>0
. By plugging in the results in Proposition
A.1
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)

\displaystyle=[1-\Phi(x)]\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{3}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mu)^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big[1+O_{1}\Big(\frac{x\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}+\frac{x^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\tau}+\frac{x^{4}}{n}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{x^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\Big)\Big]

for
2<x\leq a_{3}\min\{\tau^{3}n^{-1/2},(\sqrt{n}\tau)^{1/3},n^{1/4},\tau^{3/2}\}
. Observe that when
\tau
satisfies (
4.11
),
\tau^{3/2}\geq O(n^{1/4})
and
\frac{1+x^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\leq A\frac{(1+x)\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}
for
x=O(\sqrt{n})
. Moreover, by the basic inequality that
a+b>(a^{2}b)^{1/3}
for
a>0
and
b>0
, we have
\frac{x^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\tau}\leq\frac{x^{4}}{n}+\frac{x\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}
and
(\sqrt{n}\tau)^{1/3}\geq\min\{n^{1/4},\tau^{3}n^{-1/2}\}
.
Hence the desired result (
4.12
) holds for
x\in(2,c_{2}\min\{n^{1/4},\tau^{3}n^{-1/2}\})
, which together with the result (
A.79
) for
0<x\leq 2
completes the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

A.12
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Define
g(Y)=(Y-\mu)\mathbbm{1}\{|Y|\leq\tau\}
. Recall that

\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{S_{n}^{\circ}-\delta}{V_{n}^{\circ}}>\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x\Big),

(A.81)

where

\displaystyle S_{n}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{g(Y_{i})-\mu_{0}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{3}},\quad(V_{n}^{\circ})^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{[g(Y_{i})]^{2}}{n\sigma_{4}^{2}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\delta=\frac{\sqrt{n}\mu_{0}}{\sigma_{3}}.

The main idea is to apply Theorem
2.1
and estimate the corresponding bias-corrected term
\Psi_{x}^{*}
and error terms
L_{3,n}
and
R_{x}
under fourth moment, which are defined by

\displaystyle L_{3,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}|g(Y)-{\mu}_{0}|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{3}^{3}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}|g(Y)|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{4}^{3}}

\displaystyle\Psi_{x}^{*}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big\{(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}})^{3}x^{3}n^{-1/2}\Big(\frac{4}{3}\gamma^{3}\frac{\mathbb{E}(g(Y)-{\mu}_{0})^{3}}{\sigma_{3}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-2\gamma^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(g(Y)-{\mu}_{0})(g(Y))^{2}]}{\sigma_{3}\sigma_{4}^{2}}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle R_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}|g(Y)-{\mu}_{0}|^{4}}{\sigma_{3}^{4}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}|g(Y)|^{4}}{\sigma_{4}^{4}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{(g(Y)-{\mu}_{0})^{2}}{(g(Y))^{2}+\sigma_{4}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sigma_{4}^{2}}{\sigma_{3}^{2}}\Big\}\mathbbm{1}\big((1+x)\frac{|g(Y)-{\mu}_{0}|}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{3}}>1\big)\bigg],

where
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}(1+\frac{\delta}{x})
.

When the higher moment
\mathbb{E}[Y^{4}]<\infty
is assumed, we can figure out more accurate estimate for the bias-corrected term
\Psi_{x}^{*}
. Let us collect the bounds for some crucial quantities in the proposition below. The proof of Proposition
A.2
will be given in Section
A.17
.

Proposition A.2
.

If
\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}<\infty
, then we have there exists an absolute positive constant
A
such that

\displaystyle|\sigma_{3}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{\tau^{2}},\quad|\sigma_{4}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{\tau^{2}},\quad|\mu_{0}|\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{\tau^{3}}.

(A.82)

Moreover, when
\tau
satisfies (
4.11
), we have

\Big|\frac{\sigma_{4}^{2}}{\sigma_{3}^{2}}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}\tau^{6}},

(A.83)

and there exist absolute positive constants
A
and
a_{0}
such that

\Big|\frac{1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x+\delta\big)}{1-\Phi(x)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(1+x)\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{\sigma\tau^{3}}

(A.84)

for
0<x\leq a_{0}\frac{\tau^{3}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}
, and

R_{x}\leq\frac{A(1+x)^{4}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}{n\sigma^{4}}.

(A.85)

In addition, there exist positive constants
a_{1}
and
C
depending on
\sigma
,
\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}
and
\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}
such that

\Psi_{x}^{*}=\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{3}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mu)^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}\Big\}\Big[1+O_{1}\Big(\frac{x^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\tau}+\frac{x^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\Big)\Big]

(A.86)

for
2<x\leq a_{1}\min\{\frac{\tau^{3}}{\sqrt{n}},(\sqrt{n}\tau)^{1/3},\tau^{3/2}\}
, where
O_{1}
is a bounded quantity such that
|O_{1}|\leq C
.

We next consider two cases of
0<x\leq 2
and
x>2
, separately.
First for
0<x\leq 2
, it is immediate that
1-\Phi(x)\geq 1-\Phi(2)
. Moreover, it follows from Proposition
5.1
and (
A.84
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=[1-\Phi(x)]\Big[1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}\Big)\Big].

Since the bias term
\exp\{-\frac{x^{3}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mu)^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}\}=1+O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})
for
0<x\leq 2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=[1-\Phi(x)]\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{3}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mu)^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}\Big\}\Big[1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}\Big)\Big]

(A.87)

for
0<x\leq 2
. Next we deal with the case of
x>2
. Applying Theorem
2.1
to (
A.81
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=\Big[1-\Phi(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x+\delta)\Big]\Psi_{x}^{*}e^{O_{1}R_{x}}\Big(1+O_{2}(1+\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x)L_{3,n}\Big)

(A.88)

uniformly for
|\delta|\leq x/5
and
x>2
satisfying (
2.6
) and (
2.7
). Note that (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) are satisfied when
2<x\leq a_{2}\frac{\sqrt{n}\sigma^{4}}{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{4}}
for some constant
a_{2}>0
. By plugging in the results in Proposition
A.2
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)

\displaystyle=[1-\Phi(x)]\exp\Big\{-\frac{x^{3}\mathbb{E}(Y-\mu)^{3}}{3\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big[1+O_{1}\Big(\frac{x\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}+\frac{x^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\tau}+\frac{x^{4}}{n}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{x^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\Big)\Big]

for
2<x\leq a_{3}\min\{\tau^{3}n^{-1/2},(\sqrt{n}\tau)^{1/3},n^{1/4},\tau^{3/2}\}
. Observe that when
\tau
satisfies (
4.11
),
\tau^{3/2}\geq O(n^{1/4})
and
\frac{1+x^{2}}{\tau^{3}}\leq A\frac{(1+x)\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}
for
x=O(\sqrt{n})
. Moreover, by the basic inequality that
a+b>(a^{2}b)^{1/3}
for
a>0
and
b>0
, we have
\frac{x^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\tau}\leq\frac{x^{4}}{n}+\frac{x\sqrt{n}}{\tau^{3}}
and
(\sqrt{n}\tau)^{1/3}\geq\min\{n^{1/4},\tau^{3}n^{-1/2}\}
.
Hence the desired result holds for
x\in(2,c_{2}\min\{n^{1/4},\tau^{3}n^{-1/2}\})
, which together with the result (
A.87
) for
0<x\leq 2
completes the proof of Theorem
4.2
.

A.13
Proof of Theorem
4.3

Recall that
f(x)=x\mathbbm{1}\{|x|\leq\tau\}+\tau\mathbbm{1}\{x>\tau\}-\tau\mathbbm{1}\{x<-\tau\}
. In view of the expression shown in (
4.10
) and the notations presented above Theorem
4.1
, the main idea is to apply Theorem
2.1
and estimate the corresponding error terms
L_{3,n},\Psi_{x}^{*}
and
R_{x}
, which are defined by

\displaystyle L_{3,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-\tilde{\mu}|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}^{3}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-{\mu}|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{2}^{3}}

\displaystyle\Psi_{x}^{*}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big\{(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}})^{3}x^{3}n^{-1/2}\Big(\frac{4}{3}\gamma^{3}\frac{\mathbb{E}(f(Y)-\tilde{\mu})^{3}}{\sigma_{1}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-2\gamma^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(f(Y)-\tilde{\mu})(f(Y)-{\mu})^{2}]}{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}^{2}}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle R_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+x)^{3}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-\tilde{\mu}|^{3}}{\sigma_{1}^{3}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-{\mu}|^{3}}{\sigma_{2}^{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{(f(Y)-\tilde{\mu})^{2}}{(f(Y)-\mu)^{2}+\sigma_{2}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\Big\}\mathbbm{1}\big((1+x)\frac{|f(Y)-\tilde{\mu}|}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{1}}>1\big)\bigg],

where
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}(1+\frac{c}{x})
.

We collect the bounds for some crucial quantities in the proposition below. The proof of Proposition
A.3
will be omited since it is similar to the proof of Proposition
A.1
.

Proposition A.3
.

If
\mathbb{E}[|Y|^{3}]<\infty
, then we have there exists an absolute positive constant
A
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-\mu|^{3}\leq A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3},\quad\mathbb{E}|f(Y)-\tilde{\mu}|^{3}\leq A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}

(A.89)

\displaystyle|\sigma_{1}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\tau},\quad|\sigma_{2}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\tau},\quad|\mu-\tilde{\mu}|\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\tau^{2}}.

(A.90)

Moreover, when
\tau
satisfies (
4.14
), we have

\Big|\frac{\sigma_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}\tau^{4}},

(A.91)

and there exist absolute constants
A
and
a_{0}
such that

\Big|\frac{1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x+c\big)}{1-\Phi(x)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(1+x)\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma\tau^{2}}

(A.92)

for
0<x\leq a_{0}\frac{\tau^{2}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}
, and

R_{x}\leq\frac{A(1+x)^{3}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}}.

(A.93)

We next consider two cases of
0<x\leq 2
and
x>2
, separately.
First for
0<x\leq 2
, it follows from Proposition
5.1
that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}(S_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)-\Big[1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x+c\big)\Big]\Big|\leq AL_{3,n}.

(A.94)

From Proposition
A.3
that when
\tau
satisfies (
4.14
) with some large
a_{1}
, we can see that

\displaystyle L_{3,n}\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}},

(A.95)

and

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x+c\big)}{1-\Phi(x)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma\tau^{2}}.

(A.96)

Therefore, it holds for
0<x\leq 2
that

\frac{\mathbb{P}(S_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)}{1-\Phi(x)}=1+O_{1}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma\tau^{2}}\Big),

(A.97)

with
|O_{1}|\leq A
. The desired result (
4.15
) has been proved for
0<x\leq 2
.

Now we proceed to prove for
x>2
. Applying Theorem
2.1
to (
4.10
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=\Big[1-\Phi(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x+c)\Big]\Psi_{x}^{*}e^{O_{1}R_{x}}\Big(1+O_{2}(1+\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x)L_{3,n}\Big)

(A.98)

uniformly for
|c|\leq x/5
and
x>2
satisfying (
2.6
) and (
2.7
). When
\tau
satisfies (
4.14
), we can obtain from Proposition
A.3
that
|\sigma_{1}/\sigma_{2}-1|\leq A
and

\Big|\frac{1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}x+c\big)}{1-\Phi(x)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(1+x)\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma\tau^{2}}

(A.99)

for
x\leq a_{2}\frac{\tau^{2}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}
, and

R_{x}\leq\frac{A(1+x)^{3}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}}.

(A.100)

Moreover, since
|\frac{c}{x}|\leq 1/5
as
\tau
satisfies (
4.14
) and
x>2
, we have

\Psi_{x}^{*}\leq\exp\Big\{\frac{Ax^{3}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}}\Big\}.

(A.101)

In addition, note that the conditions of
x
shown in (
2.6
) and
(
2.7
)
are satisfied when
0<x\leq a_{2}\min\{\frac{\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}},\frac{\tau^{2}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}\}
. Consequently, the desired result (
4.15
) for
x>2
are derived by substituting (
A.95
) and (
A.99
)–(
A.101
) into (
A.98
). This completes the proof of Theorem
4.3
.

A.14
Proof of Theorem
4.4

Define
g(Y)=(Y-\mu)\mathbbm{1}\{|Y|\leq\tau\}
. Recall that

\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{S_{n}^{\circ}-\delta}{V_{n}^{\circ}}>\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x\Big),

(A.102)

where

\displaystyle S_{n}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{g(Y_{i})-\mu_{0}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{3}},\quad(V_{n}^{\circ})^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{[g(Y_{i})]^{2}}{n\sigma_{4}^{2}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\delta=\frac{\sqrt{n}\mu_{0}}{\sigma_{3}}.

The main idea is to apply Theorem
2.1
and estimate the corresponding bias-corrected term
\Psi_{x}^{*}
and error terms
L_{3,n}
and
R_{x}
under fourth moment, which are defined by

\displaystyle L_{3,n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}|g(Y)-{\mu}_{0}|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{3}^{3}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}|g(Y)|^{3}}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{4}^{3}}

\displaystyle\Psi_{x}^{*}

\displaystyle=\exp\Big\{(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}})^{3}x^{3}n^{-1/2}\Big(\frac{4}{3}\gamma^{3}\frac{\mathbb{E}(g(Y)-{\mu}_{0})^{3}}{\sigma_{3}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-2\gamma^{2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(g(Y)-{\mu}_{0})(g(Y))^{2}]}{\sigma_{3}\sigma_{4}^{2}}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle R_{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+x)^{4}}{n}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}|g(Y)-{\mu}_{0}|^{4}}{\sigma_{3}^{4}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}|g(Y)|^{4}}{\sigma_{4}^{4}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big\{\frac{(g(Y)-{\mu}_{0})^{2}}{(g(Y))^{2}+\sigma_{4}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sigma_{4}^{2}}{\sigma_{3}^{2}}\Big\}\mathbbm{1}\big((1+x)\frac{|g(Y)-{\mu}_{0}|}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_{3}}>1\big)\bigg],

where
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}(1+\frac{\delta}{x})
.

We collect the bounds for some crucial quantities in the proposition below. The proof of Proposition
A.4
will be omited since it is similar to the proof of Proposition
A.2
.

Proposition A.4
.

If
\mathbb{E}[|Y|^{3}]<\infty
, then we have there exists an absolute positive constant
A
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|g(Y)-\mu_{0}|^{3}\leq A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3},\quad\mathbb{E}|g(Y)|^{3}\leq A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3},

(A.103)

\displaystyle|\sigma_{3}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\tau},\quad|\sigma_{4}^{2}-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\tau},\quad|{\mu}_{0}|\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\tau^{2}}.

(A.104)

Moreover, when
\tau
satisfies (
4.14
), we have

\Big|\frac{\sigma_{4}^{2}}{\sigma_{3}^{2}}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}\tau^{4}},

(A.105)

and there exist absolute constants
A
and
a_{0}
such that

\Big|\frac{1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x+\delta\big)}{1-\Phi(x)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(1+x)\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma\tau^{2}}

(A.106)

for
0<x\leq a_{0}\frac{\tau^{2}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}
, and

R_{x}\leq\frac{A(1+x)^{3}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}}.

(A.107)

We next consider two cases of
0<x\leq 2
and
x>2
, separately.
First for
0<x\leq 2
, it follows from Proposition
5.1
that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)-\Big[1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x+\delta\big)\Big]\Big|\leq AL_{3,n}

(A.108)

From Proposition
A.4
that when
\tau
satisfies (
4.14
) with some large
a_{1}
, we can see that

\displaystyle L_{3,n}\leq\frac{A\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}},

(A.109)

and

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x+\delta\big)}{1-\Phi(x)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma\tau^{2}}.

(A.110)

Therefore, it holds for
0<x\leq 2
that

\frac{\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)}{1-\Phi(x)}=1+O_{1}\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma\tau^{2}}\Big),

(A.111)

with
|O_{1}|\leq A
. The desired result has been proved for
0<x\leq 2
.

Now we proceed to prove for
x>2
. Applying Theorem
2.1
to (
A.102
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(U_{\tau,n}^{*}>x)=\Big[1-\Phi(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x+\delta)\Big]\Psi_{x}^{*}e^{O_{1}R_{x}}\Big(1+O_{2}(1+\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x)L_{3,n}\Big)

(A.112)

uniformly for
|c|\leq x/5
and
x>2
satisfying (
2.6
) and (
2.7
). When
\tau
satisfies (
4.14
), we can obtain from Proposition
A.4
that
|\sigma_{3}/\sigma_{4}-1|\leq A
and

\Big|\frac{1-\Phi\big(\frac{\sigma_{4}}{\sigma_{3}}x+\delta\big)}{1-\Phi(x)}-1\Big|\leq\frac{A(1+x)\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma\tau^{2}}

(A.113)

for
x\leq a_{2}\frac{\tau^{2}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}
, and

R_{x}\leq\frac{A(1+x)^{3}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}}.

(A.114)

Moreover, since
|\frac{\delta}{x}|\leq 1/5
as
\tau
satisfies (
4.14
) and
x>2
, we have

\Psi_{x}^{*}\leq\exp\Big\{\frac{Ax^{3}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}{\sigma^{3}\sqrt{n}}\Big\}.

(A.115)

In addition, note that the conditions of
x
shown in (
2.6
) and
(
2.7
)
are satisfied when
0<x\leq a_{2}\min\{\frac{\sqrt{n}\sigma^{3}}{\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}},\frac{\tau^{2}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}|Y|^{3}}\}
. Consequently, the desired result (
4.15
) for
x>2
are derived by substituting (
A.109
) and (
A.113
)–(
A.115
) into (
A.112
). This completes the proof of Theorem
4.4
.

A.15
Proof of Theorem
4.5

Notice that
\Phi^{-1}(1-\alpha/2p)=2(1+o(1))\sqrt{\log 2p/\alpha}=o(n^{1/6})
lays in the range of Theorem
4.3
, hence by Theorem
4.3
,
